#!/usr/bin/env bash
# Copyright 2026 Anthropic PBC
# SPDX-License-Identifier: Apache-2.0
#
# Commit tracked changes at the end of every session so work is durable across
# restarts. Uses `commit -am` (tracked files only) on purpose: ephemeral
# artifacts (screenshots, logs, scratch files) shouldn't land in history. The
# agent is expected to `git add` new source files itself per CLAUDE.md.
#
# Fails silently if commit can't be made (no git user.name, hook rejects, etc);
# check `git log` periodically when relying on this as a backstop.
if git rev-parse --git-dir >/dev/null 2>&1; then
  if ! git diff --quiet || ! git diff --cached --quiet; then
    git commit -am "session checkpoint: $(date '+%Y-%m-%d %H:%M')" >/dev/null 2>&1
  fi
fi
exit 0

# Long-running conventions for this project

## Always start here
Before doing anything else, read `PROGRESS.md`. It is your handoff note from the previous session. If it doesn't exist yet, create it now with four sections (`## Done`, `## In progress`, `## Next`, `## Notes`) and leave them empty. Then run `git log --oneline -10` to see what was just committed, and run the project's smoke test (or `npm run build` / `npm test`) once so you know you're starting from a working tree, not a broken handoff.

## One feature at a time
Work on exactly one item from `PROGRESS.md` per session. Finish it (tests passing, screenshot verified) before starting another. If the user gives you a new task mid-session, add it to `PROGRESS.md` and finish the current item first.

## Proof before passing
A test is only "passing" after you have:
1. Run it against the live app (Playwright screenshot or equivalent)
2. Opened the resulting screenshot or console log with the Read tool
3. Confirmed it shows what it should

The `verify-gate` hook will deny writes to `test-results.json` until you have opened evidence. Do not try to work around it.

## Keep `PROGRESS.md` current
After each completed item, update `PROGRESS.md`: check off what's done, add what you learned, note what's next. Future sessions read this file cold.

## Commit often
The `Stop` hook commits tracked changes at session end, but also `git add` new files and commit yourself at meaningful checkpoints with descriptive messages.

## If you're told to stop
`OPERATOR STEERING:` messages come from a human via the steer hook. Treat them as higher priority than your current plan.

.claude\rules\layered-gates-architecture.md:78:Cite rationale: this matches the natural structure of Claude Code's hook event lifecycle per https://code.claude.com/docs/en/hooks (verbatim "PreToolUse: Before a tool call executes. Can block it" / "PostToolUse: After a tool call succeeds. No (post-execution)" / "Stop: When Claude finishes responding. Can block it"). Each event has a fundamentally different role in the lifecycle; conflating them produces design errors.

.claude\rules\research-protocol.md:97:Before modifying any config/state surface (`git config`, `.claude/settings.json`, `~/.codex/config.toml`, `.mcp.json`, hooks, skills, commands, symlinks): (a) STATIC PROBE — read the current value (`git config <key>`, `python -c "import json; print(json.load(...))"`, `cat <path>`, `ls -la <target>`); (b) RUNTIME PROBE when the modification affects tool semantics — apply the change locally, invoke the affected tool/parser/hook on a smoke input, capture stderr, revert if refuted. Filesystem-presence probe alone is insufficient when path resolution, env-var expansion, or flag interaction is at stake. Neither static nor runtime probe is a substitute for the other; both are required for non-trivial ships.

.claude\rules\team-orchestration.md:284:**Snapshot-labeling + recovery-pointer discipline [SSS-local]**: when a tool output is truncated under budget, the emitter MUST label the snapshot (`[truncated at N tokens]`) AND name the recovery tool to fetch the full output (`call X with id Y for complete output`). Silent truncation forces the model to infer absence vs limit. Applies to `.claude/hooks/scripts/` printing to stderr under sys.exit(2) rewake and to Agent tool results exceeding `MAX_AGENT_OUTPUT`. Writes to protected paths (`.git`, `.claude`, `.vscode`, `.idea`, `.husky`) still prompt. As of v2.1.121, writes to `.claude/commands/`, `.claude/agents/`, `.claude/skills/`, and `.claude/worktrees/` are explicitly exempt from the protected-paths prompt because Claude routinely writes there when creating skills, subagents, and commands. **v2.1.126** further extends the exemption: writes to `.claude/`, `.git/`, `.vscode/`, and shell config files (e.g., `.bashrc`, `.zshrc`) no longer prompt under `--dangerously-skip-permissions`.
